## Detailed Audit Log

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.0 (Main Text)
**English:** Show me the path that I shall follow, for it is unto thee that I have lifted up my soul (Ps. 143, [[t:0]]V[[t:1]]. 8) [Lat.: Ostende mihi viam in qua ambulabo, quia ad te levavi animam meam].
**Correction:** Show me the path that I shall follow, for it is unto thee that I have lifted up my soul (Ps. 143, [[t:0]]V[[t:1]]. 8).

**French (Source):** Fais-moi connaître le chemin que je suivrai, car c'est vers toi que j'ai élevé mon âme (Ps. 143, [[t:0]]V[[t:1]]. 8).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** הוֹדִיעֵנִי דֶּרֶךְ זוּ אֵלֵךְ, כִּי אֵלֶיךָ נָשָׂאתִי נַפְשִׁי (תהילים קמג,ח)

---

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.1 (Main Text)
**English:** It is you, men, that I call, and my voice (is addressed) to the sons of Adam (Prov. 8, 4) [Lat.: Vos autem, viri, voco, et vox mea ad filios Adam].
**Correction:** It is you, men, that I call, and my voice (is addressed) to the sons of Adam (Prov. 8, 4).

**French (Source):** C'est vous, hommes, que j'appelle, et ma voix (s'adresse) aux fils d'Adam (Prov. 8, 4).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** אֲלֵיכֶם אִישִׁים אֶקְרָא, וְקוֹלִי אֶל בְּנֵי אָדָם (משלי ח,ד)

---

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.7 (Main Text)
**English:** We have already given, in our Talmudic works[[fn:16]], several summary explanations of this kind, and drawn attention to many subjects, and we have noted that the [[t:0]]Ma'asé beréschîth[[t:1]] (account of the creation) is the physical science and the [[t:2]]Ma'asé mercabâ[[t:3]] (account of the celestial chariot) is the metaphysical science[[fn:17]], and we have expounded this sentence[[fn:18]]: 'Neither (shall one expound) the [[t:4]]mercabâ[[t:5]], even to a single (pupil), unless he be a wise man, understanding by his own [Lat.: intellect], (and in this case) one shall only impart to him the first principles.' You will therefore not ask me for anything else here but the first principles. These principles themselves are not found, in the present treatise, arranged in order and in a continuous manner, but, on the contrary, scattered and mixed with other subjects that one wished to expound; for my purpose is (to make it so) that the truths are glimpsed, and then they withdraw, in order not to be in opposition to the divine purpose, which, moreover, it would be impossible to oppose, and which has made the truths that have particularly for their object to make [Lat.: intellect] comprehend God to be hidden from the common run of men, as has said (the psalmist): [[t:6]]The secret of the Lord is for those who fear Him[[t:7]] (Ps. 25, 14).
**Correction:** We have already given, in our Talmudic works[[fn:16]], several summary explanations of this kind, and drawn attention to many subjects, and we have noted that the [[t:0]]Ma'asé beréschîth[[t:1]] (account of the creation) is the physical science and the [[t:2]]Ma'asé mercabâ[[t:3]] (account of the celestial chariot) is the metaphysical science[[fn:17]], and we have expounded this sentence[[fn:18]]: 'Neither (shall one expound) the [[t:4]]mercabâ[[t:5]], even to a single (pupil), unless he be a wise man, understanding by his own intellect, (and in this case) one shall only impart to him the first principles.' You will therefore not ask me for anything else here but the first principles. These principles themselves are not found, in the present treatise, arranged in order and in a continuous manner, but, on the contrary, scattered and mixed with other subjects that one wished to expound; for my purpose is (to make it so) that the truths are glimpsed, and then they withdraw, in order not to be in opposition to the divine purpose, which, moreover, it would be impossible to oppose, and which has made the truths that have particularly for their object to make intellect comprehend God to be hidden from the common run of men, as has said (the psalmist): [[t:6]]The secret of the Lord is for those who fear Him[[t:7]] (Ps. 25, 14).

**French (Source):** Nous avons déjà donné dans nos ouvrages talmudiques[[fn:16]] plusieurs explications sommaires de cette espèce et éveillé l'attention sur beaucoup de sujets, et nous y avons fait remarquer que le [[t:0]]Ma'asé beréschîth[[t:1]] (récit de la création) est la science physique et le [[t:2]]Ma'asé mercabâ[[t:3]] (récit du char céleste) la science métaphysique[[fn:17]], et nous avons expliqué cette sentence[[fn:18]]: «Ni (on n'interprétera) la [[t:4]]mercabâ[[t:5]], même à un (élève) seul, à moins que ce ne soit un homme sage comprenant par sa propre intelligence, (et dans ce cas) on lui en transmettra seulement les premiers éléments.» Tu ne me demanderas donc ici autre chose que les premiers éléments. Ces éléments mêmes ne se trouvent pas, dans le présent traité, rangés par ordre et d'une manière suivie, mais, au contraire, disséminés et mêlés à d'autres sujets qu'on voulait expliquer; car mon but est (de faire en sorte) que les vérités y soient entrevues, et qu'ensuite elles se dérobent, afin de ne pas être en opposition avec le but divin, auquel d'ailleurs il serait impossible de s'opposer, et qui a fait que les vérités qui ont particulièrement pour objet de faire comprendre Dieu fussent dérobées au commun des hommes, comme a dit (le psalmiste): [[t:6]]Le mystère de l'Éternel est pour ceux qui le craignent[[t:7]] (Ps. 25, 14).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <sup>5</sup> כבר ביארנו בחיבורינו ההלכתיים (פה"מ חגיגה ב,א; יסודי התורה פרקים א-ד) כללים מן הלימוד הזה והערנו על עניינים רבים, וציינו בהם ש<b>מעשה בראשית</b> הוא מדע הטבע, <b>ומעשה מרכבה</b> הוא מדע האלוהות (=המטפיזיקה), וביארנו את דבריהם "[אין דורשין בעריות בשלושה, ולא במעשֹה בראשית בשניים] <b>ולא במרכבה ביחיד אלא אם כן היה חכם ומבין מדעתו</b> [...] <b>מוסרין לו ראשי הפרקים</b>" (בבלי חגיגה יג,א). על כן אל תבקש ממני כאן יותר מ<b>ראשי הפרקים</b>. ואפילו התחלות אלו אינן מסודרות בחיבור הזה ולא רצופות אלא מפוזרות ומעורבות בעניינים אחרים שארצה לבאר. כי כוונתי היא שהאמיתות יציצו מבין הדברים ושוב ייעלמו, כדי שלא נצא נגד הכוונה האלוהית – שאין להתנגד לה – שהִסתירה מהמון בני האדם את דברי האמת המיוחדים להשגתו, שנאמר: <b>"סוֹד ה' לִירֵאָיו"</b> (תהילים כה,יד).

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.15 (Main Text)
**English:** Do you not see, moreover, that God the Most High, wishing to perfect and improve the state of our societies through His practical laws — [which can only be done after (the establishment of) certain rational dogmas, the first condition of which is to understand the divinity according to our [Lat.: facultas], which can only be done through the science of metaphysics, which in turn can only come after the science of physics; for the latter is adjacent[[fn:28]] to metaphysics and precedes it in teaching, which is clear to one who has made these studies] — (do you not see, I say,) that He has placed at the opening of His book the account of creation, which is the science of physics, as we have explained? But because of the gravity and importance of this thing, and because our [Lat.: facultas] is insufficient to understand the most serious of subjects in all its reality[[fn:29]], one has chosen, to speak to us of the profound subjects that divine wisdom judged necessary to discuss with us, allegories, enigmas, and extremely obscure words, as the doctors have said: 'To expose the power of the work of creation to mortals is impossible; this is why Scripture has spoken to you in an obscure manner: [[t:0]]In the beginning, God created, etc.[[t:1]].' They have thus warned you that the things mentioned are [[t:2]]obscure[[t:3]]. You are also familiar with these words of Solomon: [[t:4]]What exists is far[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]from our conception[[t:7]]); [[t:8]]this very profound thing, who can find it[[t:9]] (Ecclesiastes, 7, 25)? To speak of all these subjects, one has used homonymous words, so that common men could take them in a certain sense according to the measure of their intelligence and the weakness of their understanding, and so that the perfect man who has received instruction could take them in another sense.
**Correction:** Do you not see, moreover, that God the Most High, wishing to perfect and improve the state of our societies through His practical laws — [which can only be done after (the establishment of) certain rational dogmas, the first condition of which is to understand the divinity according to our faculty, which can only be done through the science of metaphysics, which in turn can only come after the science of physics; for the latter is adjacent[[fn:28]] to metaphysics and precedes it in teaching, which is clear to one who has made these studies] — (do you not see, I say,) that He has placed at the opening of His book the account of creation, which is the science of physics, as we have explained? But because of the gravity and importance of this thing, and because our faculty is insufficient to understand the most serious of subjects in all its reality[[fn:29]], one has chosen, to speak to us of the profound subjects that divine wisdom judged necessary to discuss with us, allegories, enigmas, and extremely obscure words, as the doctors have said: 'To expose the power of the work of creation to mortals is impossible; this is why Scripture has spoken to you in an obscure manner: [[t:0]]In the beginning, God created, etc.[[t:1]].' They have thus warned you that the things mentioned are [[t:2]]obscure[[t:3]]. You are also familiar with these words of Solomon: [[t:4]]What exists is far[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]from our conception[[t:7]]); [[t:8]]this very profound thing, who can find it[[t:9]] (Ecclesiastes, 7, 25)? To speak of all these subjects, one has used homonymous words, so that common men could take them in a certain sense according to the measure of their intelligence and the weakness of their understanding, and so that the perfect man who has received instruction could take them in another sense.

**French (Source):** Ne vois-tu pas d'ailleurs que Dieu le Très-Haut voulant nous perfectionner et améliorer l'état de nos sociétés par ses lois pratiques — [ce qui ne peut se faire qu'après (l'établissement de) certains dogmes rationnels dont la première condition est de comprendre la divinité selon notre faculté, chose qui ne peut avoir lieu qu'au moyen de la science métaphysique, laquelle à son tour ne peut venir qu'à la suite de la science physique; car celle-ci est limitrophe[[fn:28]] de la métaphysique et la précède dans l'enseignement, ce qui est clair pour celui qui a fait ces études] — (ne voistu pas, dis-je,) qu'il a placé à l'ouverture de son livre le récit de la création, qui est la science physique comme nous l'avons expliqué? Mais à cause de la gravité et de l'importance de cette chose, et parce que notre faculté est insuffisante pour comprendre le plus grave des sujets dans toute sa réalité[[fn:29]], on a choisi, pour nous parler des sujets profonds dont la sagesse divine jugeait nécessaire de nous entretenir, les allégories, les énigmes et des paroles extrêmement obscures, comme l'ont dit (les docteurs): «Exposer la puissance de l'œuvre de la création à des mortels est chose impossible; c'est pourquoi l'Écriture t'a dit d'une manière obscure: [[t:0]]Au commencement Dieu créa, etc[[t:1]].» Ils t'ont donc averti que les choses mentionnées sont [[t:2]]obscures[[t:3]]. Tu connais aussi ces paroles de Salomon: [[t:4]]Ce qui existe est loin[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]de notre conception[[t:7]]); [[t:8]]ce très profond, qui peut le trouver[[t:9]] (Ecclésiaste, 7, 25)? Pour parler de tous ces sujets on s'est servi de mots homonymes, afin que les hommes vulgaires pussent les prendre dans un certain sens selon la mesure de leur intelligence et la faiblesse de leur conception, et que l'homme parfait qui a reçu de l'instruction pût les prendre dans un autre sens.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">פתיחת התורה במעשי בראשית באה במשל</span><br><sup>11</sup> הלוא תראה שכאשר רצה ה', יתעלה שמו, להשלימנו ולתקן את מצבי חברתנו במצוותיו המעשיות – דבר שאינו יכול להתבצע אלא אחרי אמונות שכליות שראשיתן השגתו יתעלה כפי יכולתנו, וזה אינו יכול להתקיים אלא בחכמת האלוהות; וחכמת האלוהות הזאת אינה יכולה להיות מושגת אלא אחרי חכמת הטבע, כי חכמת הטבע גובלת בחכמת האלוהות וקודמת לה בזמן הלימוד, כמו שהתבאר למעיין בכך – פתח מן הסיבה הזאת את ספרו יתעלה ב<b>מעשה בראשית</b>, שהוא חכמת הטבע כמו שביארנו.<br><sup>12</sup> ומשום גודל העניין ונכבדותו וקוצר יכולתנו מלהשיג את הדברים הגדולים כפי שהם, נאמרו לנו הדברים העמוקים – שכורח החכמה האלוהית הביא לדבר אלינו בעניינם – במשלים ובחידות ובדברים סתומים מאוד, כמו שאמרו ז"ל: "להגיד כֹּח מעשה בראשית לבשר ודם – אי אפשר; לפיכך סתם לך הכתוב: 'בראשית ברא אלהים' וכו'" (ראו בתי מדרשות ד'). הנה העירו לך על כך שהדברים הנזכרים האלה הם סתומות. וכן יודע אתה את דברי שלמה: "[אָמַרְתִּי אֶחְכָּמָה וְהִיא רְחוֹקָה מִמֶּנִּי] רָחוֹק מַה שֶּׁהָיָה וְעָמֹק עָמֹק מִי יִמְצָאֶנּוּ" (קהלת ז,כד). בכל זה נעשה שימוש במונחים רב־משמעיים כדי שההמון יבינו אותם כפי כוח הבנתם וחולשת תפיסתם, ואילו השלם, שכבר למד, יבין אותם במשמעות אחרת.

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.23 (Main Text)
**English:** An example of the second species of prophetic allegories is found in the following passage (Prov., 7, 6 et seq.): [[t:0]]For through the window of my house, through my lattice I looked, and I saw among the simple, I discerned among the youths a child void of intellect, who, passing through the market near the corner, was advancing towards the house[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]where she dwelt[[t:3]]). [[t:4]]During the twilight, when the day was disappearing, in the darkness of the night and in the shadows. And behold, a woman comes forth to meet him in the attire of a courtesan and with a cunning heart. She is boisterous, unmanageable, etc. Now in the street, now in the squares, etc. She seizes him, etc. I had to offer peace sacrifices, etc. Therefore, I went forth to meet thee, etc. I covered my bed with tapestries, etc. I perfumed my couch, etc. Come, let us inebriate ourselves with love, etc. For the husband is not at home, etc. He has taken the purse containing the money, etc. She seduces him with the abundance of her words, she entices him with her fallacious lips[[t:5]].
**Correction:** An example of the second species of prophetic allegories is found in the following passage (Prov., 7, 6 et seq.): [[t:0]]For through the window of my house, through my lattice I looked, and I saw among the simple, I discerned among the youths a child void of intellect, who, passing through the market near the corner, was advancing towards the house[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]where she dwelt[[t:3]]). [[t:4]]During the twilight, when the day was disappearing, in the darkness of the night and in the shadows. And behold, a woman comes forth to meet him in the attire of a courtesan and with a cunning heart. She is boisterous, unmanageable, etc. Now in the street, now in the squares, etc. She seizes him, etc. I had to offer peace sacrifices, etc. Therefore, I went forth to meet thee, etc. I covered my bed with tapestries, etc. I perfumed my couch, etc. Come, let us inebriate ourselves with love, etc. For the husband is not at home, etc. He has taken the purse containing the money, etc. She seduces him with the abundance of her words, she entices him with her fallacious lips[[t:5]].

**French (Source):** Un exemple de la seconde espèce des allégories prophétiques se trouve dans le passage suivant (Prov., 7, 6 et suiv.): [[t:0]]Car par la fenêtre de ma maison, à travers mon treillis je regardais, et je vis parmi les simples, je distinguai parmi les jeunes gens un enfant dépourvu d'intelligence, qui, passant sur la place publique près d'un angle, s'avançait vers la maison[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]où elle demeurait[[t:3]]). [[t:4]]Pendant le crépuscule, quand le jour disparaissait, dans l'obscurité de la nuit et dans les ténèbres. Et voilà qu'une femme vient au devant de lui dans l'appareil d'une courtisane et le cœur rusé. Elle est bruyante, indomptée, etc. Tantôt dans la rue, tantôt sur les places, etc. Elle le saisit, etc. Je devais des sacrifices de paix, etc. C'est pourquoi je suis sortie au devant de toi, etc. J'ai couvert de tapis, etc. J'ai parfumé ma couche, etc. Viens, enivrons-nous d'amour, etc. Car l'époux n'est pas à la maison, etc. Il a pris la bourse contenant l'argent, etc. Elle le séduit par l'abondance de sa parole, elle l'entraîne par ses lèvres fallacieuses[[t:5]]. 

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">דרך ב: משל עקרוני בלבד</span><br><sup>8</sup> דוגמה למין השני של משלי הנבואה היא אמירתו "<b>כִּי בְּחַלּוֹן בֵּיתִי, בְּעַד אֶשְׁנַבִּי נִשְׁקָפְתִּי. וָאֵרֶא בַפְּתָאיִם אָבִינָה בַבָּנִים, נַעַר חֲסַר לֵב. עֹבֵר בַּשּׁוּק אֵצֶל פִּנָּהּ, וְדֶרֶךְ בֵּיתָהּ יִצְעָד. בְּנֶשֶׁף בְּעֶרֶב יוֹם, בְּאִישׁוֹן לַיְלָה וַאֲפֵלָה. וְהִנֵּה אִשָּׁה לִקְרָאתוֹ, שִׁית זוֹנָה וּנְצֻרַת לֵב. הֹמִיָּה הִיא וְסֹרָרֶת,</b> [בְּבֵיתָהּ לֹא יִשְׁכְּנוּ רַגְלֶיהָ.] <b>פַּעַם בַּחוּץ פַּעַם בָּרְחֹבוֹת,</b> [וְאֵצֶל כָּל פִּנָּה תֶאֱרֹב.] <b>וְהֶחֱזִיקָה בּוֹ</b> [וְנָשְׁקָה לּוֹ, הֵעֵזָה פָנֶיהָ וַתֹּאמַר לוֹ.] <b>זִבְחֵי שְׁלָמִים עָלָי,</b> [הַיּוֹם שִׁלַּמְתִּי נְדָרָי.] <b>עַל כֵּן יָצָאתִי לִקְרָאתֶךָ,</b> [לְשַׁחֵר פָּנֶיךָ וָאֶמְצָאֶךָּ.] <b>מַרְבַדִּים רָבַדְתִּי</b> [עַרְשִׂי, חֲטֻבוֹת אֵטוּן מִצְרָיִם.] <b>נַפְתִּי מִשְׁכָּבִי</b> [מֹר אֲהָלִים וְקִנָּמוֹן.] <b>לְכָה נִרְוֶה דֹדִים</b> [עַד הַבֹּקֶר, נִתְעַלְּסָה בָּאֳהָבִים.] <b>כִּי אֵין הָאִישׁ בְּבֵיתוֹ,</b> [הָלַךְ בְּדֶרֶךְ מֵרָחוֹק.] <b>צְרוֹר הַכֶּסֶף</b> [לָקַח בְּיָדוֹ, לְיוֹם הַכֵּסֶא יָבֹא בֵיתוֹ.] <b>הִטַּתּוּ בְּרֹב לִקְחָהּ, בְּחֵלֶק שְׂפָתֶיהָ תַּדִּיחֶנּוּ"</b> (משלי ז ו-כא).

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.25 (Main Text)
**English:** It is essential to comprehend my statements, for they are founded upon a principle of paramount importance to the explications I propose to furnish. Thus, should you perceive me elucidating the sense of a particular allégorie in a chapter of this treatise, and I direct your attention to the entirety of the thing represented, you shall not request (an explication of) every particular subject encountered within this allégorie, nor shall you endeavour to discover its analogue in the thing represented; for to do so would lead you to one of two consequences, either to lose sight of the subject which the allégorie was intended to convey, or to impose upon yourself the task of interpreting [Lat.: interpretari] words not susceptible to interpretation and not written to be [[t:0]]interpreted[[t:1]]. And in imposing this task upon yourself, you would fall prey to that great folly which afflicts the majority of the world's schools in our era, as evidenced by their writings[[fn:42]]; for each of these individuals seeks to discern a meaning in words wherein the author intended none of the significance they themselves wish to discern. You must, on the contrary, always direct your attention to comprehending the entirety of the thing intended to be conveyed in the majority of allégories, and in certain instances, it shall suffice for you to understand from my words that a particular subject is an allégorie, even if I do not expound further; for once you have ascertained that it is an allégorie, you shall immediately comprehend its object, and in stating that it is an allégorie, I shall, as it were, have removed the obstacle interposed between the viewer and the object viewed.
**Correction:** It is essential to comprehend my statements, for they are founded upon a principle of paramount importance to the explications I propose to furnish. Thus, should you perceive me elucidating the sense of a particular allégorie in a chapter of this treatise, and I direct your attention to the entirety of the thing represented, you shall not request (an explication of) every particular subject encountered within this allégorie, nor shall you endeavour to discover its analogue in the thing represented; for to do so would lead you to one of two consequences, either to lose sight of the subject which the allégorie was intended to convey, or to impose upon yourself the task of interpreting words not susceptible to interpretation and not written to be [[t:0]]interpreted[[t:1]]. And in imposing this task upon yourself, you would fall prey to that great folly which afflicts the majority of the world's schools in our era, as evidenced by their writings[[fn:42]]; for each of these individuals seeks to discern a meaning in words wherein the author intended none of the significance they themselves wish to discern. You must, on the contrary, always direct your attention to comprehending the entirety of the thing intended to be conveyed in the majority of allégories, and in certain instances, it shall suffice for you to understand from my words that a particular subject is an allégorie, even if I do not expound further; for once you have ascertained that it is an allégorie, you shall immediately comprehend its object, and in stating that it is an allégorie, I shall, as it were, have removed the obstacle interposed between the viewer and the object viewed.

**French (Source):** Il faut bien comprendre ce que je dis, car c'est un principe très important pour ce que je me propose d'expliquer. Si donc dans un chapitre de ce traité tu me vois expliquer le sens d'une certaine allégorie et que j'appelle ton attention sur l'ensemble de la chose représentée, tu ne me demanderas pas (l'explication de) tous les sujets particuliers qui se rencontrent dans cette allégorie, et tu ne chercheras pas à en trouver l'analogue dans la chose représentée; car cela te conduirait à l'une de deux choses, ou bien à te faire perdre de vue le sujet qu'on avait pour but dans l'allégorie, ou bien à t'imposer la tâche d'interpréter (allégoriquement) des paroles qui ne sont pas sujettes à interprétation et qui n'ont pas été écrites pour être [[t:0]]interprétées[[t:1]]. Et en t'imposant cette tâche tu tomberais dans cette grande folie dont la plupart des écoles du monde sont atteintes de nos jours et qui se révèle dans leurs écrits[[fn:42]]; car chacun de ces gens cherche à trouver un sens à des paroles par lesquelles l'auteur n'a visé à rien de ce qu'ils veulent (y trouver) eux-mêmes. Tu dois, au contraire, avoir toujours pour but dans la plupart des allégories de connaître l'ensemble de la chose qu'on a voulu faire connaître, et dans certaines choses il doit te suffire de comprendre par mes paroles que tel sujet est une allégorie, quand même je ne t'expliquerai rien de plus; car quand tu auras su que c'est une allégorie, tu comprendras aussitôt quel en est l'objet, et en te disant que c'est une allégorie, j'aurai, pour ainsi dire, enlevé ce qui s'interposait (comme obstacle) entre la vue et l'objet vu.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <sup>11</sup> לפיכך אם תמצאני מבאר באחד מפרקי חיבור זה את משמעותו של אחד מן המשלים ומעיר לך מהו הכלל הנמשל, אל תדרוש את כל חלקי העניינים שנאמרו במשל הזה כדי לרצות למצוא להם מקבילה בנמשל. זאת משום שהדבר יביא אותך לאחד משני דברים: או שיסיט אותך מהנושא המכוון במשל; או שיחייב אותך לפרש דברים שאין להם פירוש ושלא נועדו לפירוש. מחויבות זו תביא אותך אל כגון ההזיה העצומה הזאת שרוב כִּתות העולם בזמננו הוזות אותה ומחברות עליה חיבורים, כי כל אחת מהן רוצה שיימצא פירוש כלשהו לאמירות שהאומר אותן לא כיוון בהם לשום דבר ממה שהם (=אנשי הכתות) רוצים. אלא לעולם תהיה כוונתך ברוב המשלים לדעת את הכללות שהם כיוונו ללמד. ובחלק מהדברים יספיק לך שתבין מדבריי שסיפור פלוני הוא משל גם אם לא נבאר יותר מכך. משום שכאשר תדע שזה משל יתבאר לך מיד לאיזה דבר הוא משל, ואמירתי שהוא משל תהיה כמי שהסיר דבר החוצץ בין כוח הראייה לבין הנראה.

---

### root.text.Prefatory Remarks.26 (Main Text)
**English:** [[t:0]]RECOMMENDATION REGARDING THIS TREATISE.[[t:1]] [[t:2]]If you wish to comprehend all that (this treatise) contains, in such a manner that nothing escapes you, it is necessary to combine its chapters with one another, and in reading a chapter, you must not only aim to understand the entirety of its subject, but also to grasp each word that presents itself in the sequence of the discourse, even if it does not pertain to the principal subject of the chapter. For in this treatise, I have never spoken [Lat.: fortuitously], but (all has been said) with great exactitude and with much precision, and with care to leave no explanation of any obscurity wanting; and if something has been said (here and there) that is not in its place, it has been only to explain something else in its true place. You must not approach it with your preconceived opinions, for you would do me an injustice without profiting yourself; but, on the contrary, you must first learn all that it is necessary to learn. You will make it the object of your continual studies, for it will explain to you the most grave obscurities of the Law, which are difficult even for every intelligent man. I implore, by God the Most High, all those who will have read the present treatise, not to comment on a single word of it, nor to explain anything of it to others, unless it be what is already clearly explained in the words of those who have preceded me among the celebrated doctors of our Law. But (the reader) who will have understood something of what others of our celebrated men have not said, must not explain it to others; nor must he hasten to refute me[[fn:43]]; for it may be that what he will have understood in my words was the contrary of what I intended to say, and then he would do me an injustice in return for what I intended to be useful to him, and [[t:3]]he would repay good with evil[[t:4]]. On the contrary, he to whom (this treatise) will have fallen into the hands, must study it with care, and if he finds in it something to satisfy his desire[[fn:44]], be it only on a single point of all that is obscure, he must thank God and be content with what he has understood. If he finds in it absolutely nothing that is useful to him, he must consider it as if it had never been composed; but if it seems to him to result in harm with regard to his opinions, he must interpret (my words) [[t:5]]in a manner that judges me favourably[[t:6]] ([[t:7]]ידין לכף זכות[[t:8]]), even by the most recherché interpretation, as it has been prescribed to us with regard to our common people, and all the more so with regard to our scholars and the supporters of our Law, who strive to teach us the truth according to their faculty.
**Correction:** [[t:0]]RECOMMENDATION REGARDING THIS TREATISE.[[t:1]] [[t:2]]If you wish to comprehend all that (this treatise) contains, in such a manner that nothing escapes you, it is necessary to combine its chapters with one another, and in reading a chapter, you must not only aim to understand the entirety of its subject, but also to grasp each word that presents itself in the sequence of the discourse, even if it does not pertain to the principal subject of the chapter. For in this treatise, I have never spoken fortuitously, but (all has been said) with great exactitude and with much precision, and with care to leave no explanation of any obscurity wanting; and if something has been said (here and there) that is not in its place, it has been only to explain something else in its true place. You must not approach it with your preconceived opinions, for you would do me an injustice without profiting yourself; but, on the contrary, you must first learn all that it is necessary to learn. You will make it the object of your continual studies, for it will explain to you the most grave obscurities of the Law, which are difficult even for every intelligent man. I implore, by God the Most High, all those who will have read the present treatise, not to comment on a single word of it, nor to explain anything of it to others, unless it be what is already clearly explained in the words of those who have preceded me among the celebrated doctors of our Law. But (the reader) who will have understood something of what others of our celebrated men have not said, must not explain it to others; nor must he hasten to refute me[[fn:43]]; for it may be that what he will have understood in my words was the contrary of what I intended to say, and then he would do me an injustice in return for what I intended to be useful to him, and [[t:3]]he would repay good with evil[[t:4]]. On the contrary, he to whom (this treatise) will have fallen into the hands, must study it with care, and if he finds in it something to satisfy his desire[[fn:44]], be it only on a single point of all that is obscure, he must thank God and be content with what he has understood. If he finds in it absolutely nothing that is useful to him, he must consider it as if it had never been composed; but if it seems to him to result in harm with regard to his opinions, he must interpret (my words) [[t:5]]in a manner that judges me favourably[[t:6]] ([[t:7]]ידין לכף זכות[[t:8]]), even by the most recherché interpretation, as it has been prescribed to us with regard to our common people, and all the more so with regard to our scholars and the supporters of our Law, who strive to teach us the truth according to their faculty.

**French (Source):** [[t:0]]RECOMMANDATION AU SUJET DE CE TRAITÉ.[[t:1]] [[t:2]]Si tu veux comprendre tout ce que (ce traité) renferme, de manière à ce qu'il ne t'en échappe rien, il faut combiner ses chapitres les uns avec les autres, et en lisant un chapitre il ne faut pas seulement avoir pour but de comprendre l'ensemble de son sujet, mais aussi de saisir chaque parole qui s'y présente dans la suite du discours, quand même elle ne ferait pas partie du sujet (principal) du chapitre. Car dans ce traité il ne m'est jamais arrivé de parler comme par hasard, mais (tout a été dit) avec une grande exactitude et avec beaucoup de précision, et en ayant soin de n'y laisser manquer l'explication d'aucune obscurité; et si on y a dit (çà et là) quelque chose qui n'est pas à sa place, ce n'a été que pour expliquer quelque autre chose à sa véritable place. Il ne faut donc pas l'aborder avec tes opinions préconçues, car tu me ferais du tort sans en profiter toi-même; mais il faut, au contraire, que tu apprennes d'abord tout ce qu'il est nécessaire d'apprendre. Tu en feras l'objet continuel de tes études, car il t'expliquera les plus graves obscurités de la Loi, qui sont difficiles même pour tout homme intelligent. J'en conjure par Dieu le Très-Haut tous ceux qui auront lu le présent traité de ne pas en commenter un seul mot et de n'en rien expliquer aux autres, si ce n'est ce qui est déjà clairement expliqué dans les paroles de ceux qui m'ont précédé d'entre les célèbres docteurs de notre Loi. Mais (le lecteur) qui en aura compris quelque chose de ce que d'autres de nos hommes célèbres n'ont pas dit ne devra pas l'expliquer à d'autres; il ne devra pas non plus se presser de me réfuter[[fn:43]]; car il se pourrait que ce qu'il aura compris dans mes paroles fût le contraire de ce que j'ai voulu dire, et alors il me ferait du tort en retour de ce que moi j'ai voulu lui être utile, et [[t:3]]il paierait le bien par le mal[[t:4]]. Il faut, au contraire, que celui à qui (ce traité) tombera entre les mains l'étudie avec soin, et s'il y trouve de quoi satisfaire son désir[[fn:44]], ne fût-ce que sur un seul point de tout ce qui est obscur, il doit remercier Dieu et se contenter de ce qu'il en a compris. S'il n'y trouve absolument rien qui lui soit utile, il doit le considérer comme s'il n'avait jamais été composé; mais s'il lui semble en résulter un dommage par rapport à ses opinions, il doit interpréter (mes paroles) [[t:5]]de manière à me juger favorablement[[t:6]] ([[t:7]]ידין לכף זכות[[t:8]]), fût-ce même par l'interprétation la plus recherchée, comme cela nous a été prescrit à l'égard de nos gens du vulgaire et à plus forte raison à l'égard de nos savants et des soutiens de notre Loi, qui s'efforcent de nous enseigner la vérité selon leur faculté.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** [הוראות (ללימוד) חיבור זה]<br><span class="mediumGrey">הגישה הראויה לספר</span><br><sup>1</sup> אם תרצה להשיג את כל מה שכללתי בו כך שלא יחסר לך ממנו דבר, השווה את פרקיו זה עם זה; ולא תהא כוונתך בכל פרק להבין את משמעותו הכללית בלבד, אלא גם להשיג כל ביטוי שנאמר בו תוך כדי הדברים, גם אם אינו ממטרת הפרק. משום שבחיבור זה הדברים לא באו כפי שיזדמן, אלא בדקדוק גדול ובדיוק רב ותוך זהירות מלפגוע בביאור קשיים, ולא נאמר בו דבר שלא במקומו אלא כדי לבאר עניין מסוים במקומו. אל תגרור אותו אחר דמיונות השווא שלך, משום שאז תזיק לי ולא תועיל לעצמך. אלא ראוי לך שתלמד כל מה שראוי שתלמדהו, ועיין בו תמיד, כי הוא יבאר לך את רוב הקשיים בתורה, שכל משכיל מתקשה בהם.<br><span class="mediumGrey">השבעת הקורא שלא להזיק למחבר</span><br><sup>2</sup> משביע אני בה' יתעלה את כל מי שיקרא את חיבורי זה, <br><sup>א)</sup> שלא יפרש ממנו אפילו מילה אחת, ולא יבאר לאחרים ממנו אלא מה שהוא מבואר ומפורש בדברי מי שקדם לי מחכמי תורתנו המפורסמים. ואילו מה שיבין ממנו שלא נאמר על ידי מי מן המפורסמים אצלנו, לא יבאר לאחרים, <br><sup>ב)</sup> ולא ייחפז לטעון נגדי. כי אפשר שמה שהבין מדבריי יהיה שונה ממה שהתכוונתי אליו, ויגרום לי נזק כגמול על רצוני להיטיב לו, ויהיה <b>משלם רעה תחת טובה</b> (ראו תהילים לח,כא; משלי יז,יג).<br><sup>3</sup> אלא יתבונן בו כל מי שייפול לידיו, ואם ירווה את צמאונו ולוּ בעניין כלשהו מכלל מה שיתקשה בו, יודה לה' ויסתפק במה שהבין. ואם לא ימצא בו כלל דבר שיועיל לו, יחשוב אותו כאילו לא חובר. ואם ייראה לו בו פגם כלשהו לפי סברתו, יפרשנו (שלא כפשוטו) אפילו בפירוש דחוק ביותר ו<b>ידין לכף זכות</b> (ראו משנה אבות א,ו), כמו שנצטווינו באשר להמוני בני עמנו, כל שכן לגבי מלומדינו וחכמי תורתנו המשתדלים ללמדנו את האמת כפי השגתם.

---

### root.text.Part 1.Introduction.1 (Main Text)
**English:** F[Lat.: prima]IRST CAUSE[[t:1]]: When the author has gathered the words of people of different opinions, omitting (to cite) the authorities and to attribute each word to its author. One then finds in his work contradictions or opposed assertions, because one of the two propositions is the opinion of one individual, and the other the opinion of another individual.
**Correction:** FIRST CAUSE[[t:1]]: When the author has gathered the words of people of different opinions, omitting (to cite) the authorities and to attribute each word to its author. One then finds in his work contradictions or opposed assertions, because one of the two propositions is the opinion of one individual, and the other the opinion of another individual.

**French (Source):** P[[t:0]]REMIÈRE CAUSE[[t:1]]: Quand l'auteur a rassemblé les paroles de gens d'opinions différentes en omettant (de citer) les autorités et d'attribuer chaque parole à son auteur. On trouve alors dans son ouvrage des contradictions ou des assertions opposées, parce que l'une des deux propositions est l'opinion d'un individu, et l'autre l'opinion d'un autre individu.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <sup>2</sup> הסיבה הראשונה היא שהמחבר אסף אמירות של אנשים בעלי דעות שונות, והשמיט את שם האומר ולא ייחס כל אמירה לאומרהּ. בחיבור זה יימצאו סתירה או ניגוד כאשר משפט אחד הוא שיטתו של אדם אחד והמשפט האחר הוא שיטתו של אחר.

---

### root.text.Part 1.Introduction.3 (Main Text)
**English:** T[[t:0]]HIRD CAUSE[[t:1]]: When the words (of the author) are not all (taken) in their external [Lat.: exterior] sense (literal); but some retain their literal sense and others are an image and have a figurative sense, or when the two contradictory propositions, according to their literal sense, are allegories, and when, taken in their literal sense, they appear contradictory or opposed to one another.
**Correction:** T[[t:0]]HIRD CAUSE[[t:1]]: When the words (of the author) are not all (taken) in their external sense (literal); but some retain their literal sense and others are an image and have a figurative sense, or when the two contradictory propositions, according to their literal sense, are allegories, and when, taken in their literal sense, they appear contradictory or opposed to one another.

**French (Source):** T[[t:0]]ROISIÈME CAUSE[[t:1]]: Lorsque les paroles (de l'auteur) ne sont pas toutes (prises) dans leur sens extérieur (littéral); mais que les unes conservent leur sens littéral et que les autres sont une image et ont un sens figuré, ou bien que les deux propositions, contradictoires selon leur sens littéral, sont des allégories, et que, prises dans leur sens littéral, elles paraissent contradictoires ou opposées entre elles.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <sup>4</sup> הסיבה השלישית: כאשר לא כל הדברים הללו הם כחיצוניותם, אלא חלקם כחיצוניותם וחלקם משל שיש לו תוך, או ששני המשפטים הנראים כסותרים הם משלים, אלא שאם הובנו לפי פשוטם הרי הם סותרים או מנוגדים.

---

### root.text.Part 1..0.0 (Main Text)
**English:** [Lat.: Aperite portas, ut ingrediatur gens justa, quae servavit fidem (Isaiah, xxvi, 2)].
**Correction:** Open the gates, that the righteous nation which keepeth the truth may enter in.

**French (Source):** OUVREZ LES PORTES, QUE LE PEUPLE JUSTE ENTRE, LUI QUI GARDE LA FOI (Isaïe, xxvi, 2).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <b>פִּתְחוּ שְׁעָרִים וְיָבֹא גוֹי צַדִּיק שֹׁמֵר אֱמֻנִים</b> (ישעיהו כו,ב)

---

### root.text.Part 1..1.4.sub_0 (Main Text)
**English:** Hear now in what manner we responded. O you, we said, who examine (things) with a superficial and unreflecting spirit[[fn:70]], and who believe you understand a book, a guide for the ancients and the moderns, by perusing it in a few moments of leisure (stolen from the pleasures) of drinking and cohabitation, as one would peruse some historical book or poem! Stop and examine; for the thing is not as you believed it to be at first glance, but as it shall manifest itself when one has considered what I am about to say. The reason that God has caused to emanate upon man, and which constitutes his final perfection, is that which Adam possessed before his disobedience; it is for this that he was said to be (made) [[t:0]]in the image of God and in His likeness[[t:1]], and it is on account of this that the word was addressed to him and he received commands, as it is said (in Scripture): [[t:2]]And the Lord God commanded[[t:3]], etc. (Genesis, 2, 16), for one cannot give commands to animals or to one who has no reason. By reason one distinguishes between the [[t:4]]true[[t:5]] and the [[t:6]]false[[t:7]], and this faculty he (Adam) possessed perfectly and completely; but the [[t:8]]ugly[[t:9]] and the [[t:10]]beautiful[[t:11]] exist in (matters of) [[t:12]]probable opinions[[t:13]], and not in (things that are) [[t:14]]intelligible[[t:15]][[fn:71]]; for one does not say that this proposition: [[t:16]]the heaven is spherical[[t:17]], is beautiful, nor that this other: [[t:18]]the earth is flat[[t:19]], is ugly; but one calls the one [[t:20]]true[[t:21]] and the other [[t:22]]false[[t:23]]. Thus in our language we employ (in speaking of) the [[t:24]]true[[t:25]] and the [[t:26]]false[[t:27]] (the words) [[t:28]]אמת[[t:29]] and [[t:30]]שקר[[t:31]], and for [[t:32]]beautiful[[t:33]] and [[t:34]]ugly[[t:35]] (we say) [[t:36]]טוב[[t:37]] and [[t:38]]רע[[t:39]]. By reason, therefore, man distinguishes the [[t:40]]true[[t:41]] from the [[t:42]]false[[t:43]], and this takes place in all (things that are) intelligible. When, therefore, he (Adam) was still in his most perfect and complete state, having only his primitive nature and his intelligible notions, on account of which it was said of him: [[t:44]]And You have made him a little lower than the angels[[t:45]] (Psalm [Lat.: Psalmos]),
**Correction:** Hear now in what manner we responded. O you, we said, who examine (things) with a superficial and unreflecting spirit[[fn:70]], and who believe you understand a book, a guide for the ancients and the moderns, by perusing it in a few moments of leisure (stolen from the pleasures) of drinking and cohabitation, as one would peruse some historical book or poem! Stop and examine; for the thing is not as you believed it to be at first glance, but as it shall manifest itself when one has considered what I am about to say. The reason that God has caused to emanate upon man, and which constitutes his final perfection, is that which Adam possessed before his disobedience; it is for this that he was said to be (made) [[t:0]]in the image of God and in His likeness[[t:1]], and it is on account of this that the word was addressed to him and he received commands, as it is said (in Scripture): [[t:2]]And the Lord God commanded[[t:3]], etc. (Genesis, 2, 16), for one cannot give commands to animals or to one who has no reason. By reason one distinguishes between the [[t:4]]true[[t:5]] and the [[t:6]]false[[t:7]], and this faculty he (Adam) possessed perfectly and completely; but the [[t:8]]ugly[[t:9]] and the [[t:10]]beautiful[[t:11]] exist in (matters of) [[t:12]]probable opinions[[t:13]], and not in (things that are) [[t:14]]intelligible[[t:15]][[fn:71]]; for one does not say that this proposition: [[t:16]]the heaven is spherical[[t:17]], is beautiful, nor that this other: [[t:18]]the earth is flat[[t:19]], is ugly; but one calls the one [[t:20]]true[[t:21]] and the other [[t:22]]false[[t:23]]. Thus in our language we employ (in speaking of) the [[t:24]]true[[t:25]] and the [[t:26]]false[[t:27]] (the words) [[t:28]]אמת[[t:29]] and [[t:30]]שקר[[t:31]], and for [[t:32]]beautiful[[t:33]] and [[t:34]]ugly[[t:35]] (we say) [[t:36]]טוב[[t:37]] and [[t:38]]רע[[t:39]]. By reason, therefore, man distinguishes the [[t:40]]true[[t:41]] from the [[t:42]]false[[t:43]], and this takes place in all (things that are) intelligible. When, therefore, he (Adam) was still in his most perfect and complete state, having only his primitive nature and his intelligible notions, on account of which it was said of him: [[t:44]]And You have made him a little lower than the angels[[t:45]] (Psalm),

**French (Source):** Écoute maintenant de quelle manière nous y avons répondu. O toi, disions-nous, qui examines (les choses) avec un esprit superficiel et irréfléchi[[fn:70]], et qui crois comprendre un livre, guide des anciens et des modernes, en le parcourant dans quelques moments de loisir (dérobés aux plaisirs) de la boisson et de la cohabitation, comme on parcourrait quelque livre d’histoire ou quelque poëme! arrête-toi et examine; car la chose n’est pas telle que tu la croyais au premier abord, mais telle qu’elle se manifestera quand on aura considéré ce que je vais dire. La raison que Dieu a fait émaner sur l’homme, et qui constitue sa perfection finale, est celle qu’Adam possédait avant sa désobéissance; c’est pour elle qu’il a été dit de lui qu’il était (fait) [[t:0]]à l’image de Dieu et à sa ressemblance[[t:1]], et c’est à cause d’elle que la parole lui fut adressée et qu’il reçut des ordres, comme dit (l’Écriture): [[t:2]]Et l’Éternel, Dieu ordonna[[t:3]], etc. (Genèse, 2, 16), car on ne peut pas donner d’ordres aux animaux ni à celui qui n’a pas de raison. Par la raison on distingue entre le [[t:4]]vrai[[t:5]] et le [[t:6]]faux[[t:7]], et cette faculté il (Adam) la possédait parfaitement et complétement; mais le [[t:8]]laid[[t:9]] et le [[t:10]]beau[[t:11]] existent dans les (choses des) [[t:12]]opinions probables[[t:13]], et non dans les [[t:14]]choses intelligibles[[t:15]][[fn:71]]; car on ne dit pas que cette proposition: [[t:16]]le ciel est sphérique[[t:17]], soit belle, ni que cette autre: [[t:18]]la terre est plane[[t:19]], soit laide; mais on appelle l’une [[t:20]]vraie[[t:21]] et l’autre [[t:22]]fausse.[[t:23]] Ainsi dans notre langue on emploie (en parlant) du [[t:24]]vrai[[t:25]] et du [[t:26]]faux[[t:27]] (les mots) [[t:28]]émeth[[t:29]] ([[t:30]]אמת[[t:31]]) et [[t:32]]schéker[[t:33]] ([[t:34]]שקר[[t:35]]), et pour [[t:36]]beau[[t:37]] et [[t:38]]laid[[t:39]] (on dit) [[t:40]]tôb[[t:41]] ([[t:42]]טוב[[t:43]]) et [[t:44]]ra’[[t:45]] ([[t:46]]רע[[t:47]]). Par la raison donc l’homme distingue le [[t:48]]vrai[[t:49]] du [[t:50]]faux[[t:51]], et ceci a lieu dans toutes les choses intelligibles. Lors donc qu’il (Adam) était encore dans son état le plus parfait et le plus complet, n’ayant que sa nature primitive et ses notions intelligibles, à cause desquelles il a été dit de lui: [[t:52]]Et tu l’as placé peu au dessous des êtres divins[[t:53]] (Ps.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** [Not Found]

### root.text.Part 1..2.0 (Main Text)
**English:** One imagines that the meaning of [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]) and of [[t:4]]tabnîth[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תבנית[[t:7]]) in the Hebrew language is the same; but such is not the case. [[t:8]]Tabnîth[[t:9]] is a noun derived from [[t:10]]בנה[[t:11]] (to build) and signifies the [[t:12]]structure[[t:13]] of a thing and its [Lat.: forma], i.e., its figure, such as, for example, a square, circular, triangular figure, etc. It is said, for example: [[t:14]]The figure[[t:15]] ([[t:16]]תבנית[[t:17]]) [[t:18]]of the tabernacle and the figure[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]תבנית[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]of all its vessels[[t:23]] (Exod., 25, 9), and it is said again: [[t:24]]According to their figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]כתבניתם[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]which was shown thee on the mount[[t:29]] ([[t:30]]Ibid.[[t:31]], [[t:32]]V[[t:33]]. 40); [[t:34]]The figure[[t:35]] ([[t:36]]תבנית[[t:37]]) [[t:38]]of every bird[[t:39]] (Deut., 4, 17); [[t:40]]The figure[[t:41]] ([[t:42]]תבנית[[t:43]]) [[t:44]]of a hand[[t:45]] (Ezek., 8, 3); [[t:46]]The figure[[t:47]] ([[t:48]]תבנית[[t:49]]) [[t:50]]of the porch[[t:51]] (I Chron., 28, 11). All this is a [[t:52]]figure[[t:53]] ([[t:54]]visible[[t:55]]); this is why the Hebrew language does not employ this kind of expression in descriptions that relate to God, [Lat.: intellectus] and [Lat.: facultas] being distinct from [Lat.: forma] and [Lat.: materia].
**Correction:** One imagines that the meaning of [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]) and of [[t:4]]tabnîth[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תבנית[[t:7]]) in the Hebrew language is the same; but such is not the case. [[t:8]]Tabnîth[[t:9]] is a noun derived from [[t:10]]בנה[[t:11]] (to build) and signifies the [[t:12]]structure[[t:13]] of a thing and its form, i.e., its figure, such as, for example, a square, circular, triangular figure, etc. It is said, for example: [[t:14]]The figure[[t:15]] ([[t:16]]תבנית[[t:17]]) [[t:18]]of the tabernacle and the figure[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]תבנית[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]of all its vessels[[t:23]] (Exod., 25, 9), and it is said again: [[t:24]]According to their figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]כתבניתם[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]which was shown thee on the mount[[t:29]] ([[t:30]]Ibid.[[t:31]], [[t:32]]V[[t:33]]. 40); [[t:34]]The figure[[t:35]] ([[t:36]]תבנית[[t:37]]) [[t:38]]of every bird[[t:39]] (Deut., 4, 17); [[t:40]]The figure[[t:41]] ([[t:42]]תבנית[[t:43]]) [[t:44]]of a hand[[t:45]] (Ezek., 8, 3); [[t:46]]The figure[[t:47]] ([[t:48]]תבנית[[t:49]]) [[t:50]]of the porch[[t:51]] (I Chron., 28, 11). All this is a [[t:52]]figure[[t:53]] ([[t:54]]visible[[t:55]]); this is why the Hebrew language does not employ this kind of expression in descriptions that relate to God,intellect and faculty being distinct from form and matter.

**French (Source):** On s’imagine que le sens de [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]) et de [[t:4]]tabnîth[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תבנית[[t:7]]) dans la langue hébraïque est le même; mais il n’en est pas ainsi. [[t:8]]Tabnîth[[t:9]] est un nom dérivé de [[t:10]]בנה[[t:11]] (bâtir) et signifie la [[t:12]]bâtisse[[t:13]] d’une chose et sa structure, je veux dire sa figure, comme p. ex. la figure carrée, circulaire, triangulaire, etc. On dit, p. ex.: [[t:14]]La figure[[t:15]] ([[t:16]]תבנית[[t:17]]) [[t:18]]du tabernacle et la figure[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]תבנית[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]de tous ses ustensiles[[t:23]] (Exod., 25, 9), et on dit encore: [[t:24]]Selon leur figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]כתבניתם[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]qui t’a été montrée sur la montagne[[t:29]] ([[t:30]]Ibid.[[t:31]], [[t:32]]V[[t:33]]. 40); [[t:34]]La figure[[t:35]] ([[t:36]]תבנית[[t:37]]) [[t:38]]de tout oiseau[[t:39]] (Deutéron., 4, 17); [[t:40]]La figure[[t:41]] ([[t:42]]תבנית[[t:43]]) [[t:44]]d’une main[[t:45]] (Ézéch., 8, 3); [[t:46]]La figure[[t:47]] ([[t:48]]תבנית[[t:49]]) [[t:50]]du portique[[t:51]] (I Chron., 28, 11). Tout cela est une [[t:52]]figure[[t:53]] ([[t:54]]visible[[t:55]]); c’est pourquoi la langue hébraïque n’emploie aucunement cette sorte d’expressions dans des descriptions qui se rapportent à Dieu.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <b>המונחים תבנית ותמונה</b><br><span class="mediumGrey">משמעות המונח תבנית</span><br><sup>1</sup> חושבים שמשמעות "<b>תמונה</b>" ו"<b>תבנית</b>" בלשון העברית אחת היא, ואין הדבר כן. משום ש"<b>תבנית</b>" הוא מונח הגזור מן "<b>בנה</b>", ומשמעותו מבנה הדבר ותכונתו, כלומר תבניתו, דוגמת הריבועיוּת והעיגוליוּת והמשולשוּת ושאר התבניות. נאמר: "<b>אֵת תַּבְנִית הַמִּשְׁכָּן וְאֵת תַּבְנִית כָּל כֵּלָיו</b>" (שמות כה,ט), ונאמר: "[וּרְאֵה וַעֲשֵׂה] <b>בְּתַבְנִיתָם אֲשֶׁר אַתָּה מָרְאֶה בָּהָר</b>" (שם,מ), "<b>תַּבְנִית כָּל צִפּוֹר [</b>כָּנָף אֲשֶׁר תָּעוּף בַּשָּׁמָיִם]" (דברים ד,יז), "<b>תַּבְנִית יָד</b>" (יחזקאל ח,ג; י,ח), "<b>תַּבְנִית הָאוּלָם</b>" (דברי הימים־א כח,יא). כל אלה – תבנית. ולכן אין העברית מחילה כלל את הביטויים הללו על תיאורים הקשורים באלוה.

### root.text.Part 1..2.1 (Main Text)
**English:** With regard to [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]), it is a term that is employed in three distinct senses by way of [Lat.: amphibologia]. Firstly, it denotes the [Lat.: forma] of an object perceived by the senses independently of the [Lat.: intellectus], that is to say, its figure and lineaments. This is the sense in which the words are used: [[t:4]]….. and you shall make a carved image of the figure[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תמונת[[t:7]]) [[t:8]]of anything, etc.[[t:9]] (Deuteronomy, 4, 25); [[t:10]]For you saw no [Lat.: forma][[t:11]] ([[t:12]]תמונה[[t:13]]) ([[t:14]]Ibid.[[t:15]], [[t:16]]V[[t:17]]. 15). Secondly, it denotes the imaginary figure that an object, after having withdrawn from the senses, leaves in the [Lat.: facultas imaginativa], as in this passage: [[t:18]]In thoughts[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]nées[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]of nocturnal visions, etc.[[t:23]] (Job, 4, 13 et seq.), which concludes with these words: [[t:24]]It stood still and I did not recognize its face; there was a figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]תמונה[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]before my eyes[[t:29]], that is to say, there was a specter before my eyes in sleep. Lastly, it denotes the true [Lat.: idea] (of a thing) perceived by the [Lat.: intellectus], and it is in this third sense that one speaks of [[t:30]]temounâ[[t:31]] in reference to God; for example: [[t:32]]And he contemplates the [Lat.: forma][[t:33]] ([[t:34]]תמונת[[t:35]]) [[t:36]]of the Eternal[[t:37]] (Numbers, 12, 8), which must be explained in this sense: [[t:38]]And he comprehends God in His reality[[t:39]].
**Correction:** With regard to [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]), it is a term that is employed in three distinct senses by way of amphibology. Firstly, it denotes the form of an object perceived by the senses independently of the intellect, that is to say, its figure and lineaments. This is the sense in which the words are used: [[t:4]]….. and you shall make a carved image of the figure[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תמונת[[t:7]]) [[t:8]]of anything, etc.[[t:9]] (Deuteronomy, 4, 25); [[t:10]]For you saw no form[[t:11]] ([[t:12]]תמונה[[t:13]]) ([[t:14]]Ibid.[[t:15]], [[t:16]]V[[t:17]]. 15). Secondly, it denotes the imaginary figure that an object, after having withdrawn from the senses, leaves in the imaginative faculty, as in this passage: [[t:18]]In thoughts[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]nées[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]of nocturnal visions, etc.[[t:23]] (Job, 4, 13 et seq.), which concludes with these words: [[t:24]]It stood still and I did not recognize its face; there was a figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]תמונה[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]before my eyes[[t:29]], that is to say, there was a specter before my eyes in sleep. Lastly, it denotes the true idea (of a thing) perceived by the intellect, and it is in this third sense that one speaks of [[t:30]]temounâ[[t:31]] in reference to God; for example: [[t:32]]And he contemplates the form[[t:33]] ([[t:34]]תמונת[[t:35]]) [[t:36]]of the Eternal[[t:37]] (Numbers, 12, 8), which must be explained in this sense: [[t:38]]And he comprehends God in His reality[[t:39]].

**French (Source):** Quant à [[t:0]]temounâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]תמונה[[t:3]]), c’est un nom qui se dit par amphibologie[[fn:76]] dans trois sens divers. Il se dit 1° de la forme d’un objet perçue par les sens indépendamment de l’esprit, je veux dire de sa figure et de ses linéaments, et c’est là le sens des mots: [[t:4]]….. et que vous ferez une image taillée de la figure[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]תמונת[[t:7]]) [[t:8]]de quoi que ce soit, etc.[[t:9]] (Deutéron., 4, 25); [[t:10]]Car vous n’avez vu aucune figure[[t:11]] ([[t:12]]תמונה[[t:13]]) ([[t:14]]Ibid.[[t:15]], [[t:16]]V[[t:17]]. 15). On le dit 2° de la figure imaginaire qu’un objet, après s’être dérobé aux sens, laisse dans l’imagination, comme dans ce passage: [[t:18]]Dans les pensées[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]nées[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]de visions noctures, etc.[[t:23]] (Job, 4, 13 et suiv.), qui finit par ces mots: [[t:24]]Il s’arrêta et je ne reconnaissais pas son visage; il y avait une figure[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]תמונה[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]devant mes yeux[[t:29]], c’est-à-dire il y avait un fantôme devant mes yeux dans le sommeil. On le dit enfin 3° de l’idée véritable (d’une chose) perçue par l’intelligence, et c’est dans ce troisième sens qu’on dit [[t:30]]temounâ[[t:31]] en parlant de Dieu; p. ex.: [[t:32]]Et il contemple la figure[[t:33]] ([[t:34]]תמונת[[t:35]]) [[t:36]]de l’Éternel[[t:37]] (Nombres, 12, 8), ce qui doit être expliqué dans ce sens: [[t:38]]Et il comprend Dieu dans sa réalité[[t:39]].

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">שלוש משמעויות למונח תמונה</span><br><sup>2</sup> ואילו "<b>תמונה</b>" היא מונח מסופק בעל שלוש משמעויות: <br><sup>א)</sup> הוא משמש במובן של צורת הדבר המושגת בחושים מחוץ לדעת (=באופן אובייקטיבי), כלומר תבניתו ומתארו, והם דבריו: "<b>וַעֲשִׂיתֶם פֶּסֶל תְּמוּנַת כֹּל</b>" (דברים ד,כה), "<b>כִּי לֹא רְאִיתֶם כָּל תְּמוּנָה</b> [בְּיוֹם דִּבֶּר ה' אֲלֵיכֶם בְּחֹרֵב מִתּוֹךְ הָאֵשׁ]" (שם,טו). <br><sup>ב)</sup> הוא משמש במובן של הצורה המדומיינת של הפרט, הנמצאת בדמיון לאחר היעלמו מן החושים, והוא שנאמר: "<b>בִּשְׂעִפִּים מֵחֶזְיֹנוֹת לָיְלָה [</b>בִּנְפֹל תַּרְדֵּמָה עַל אֲנָשִׁים]" (איוב ד,יג), וסוף הדברים: "<b>יַעֲמֹד וְלֹא אַכִּיר מַרְאֵהוּ, תְּמוּנָה לְנֶגֶד עֵינָי</b>" (שם,טז), כלומר דמיון אל מול עיניי בשנתי. <br><sup>ג)</sup> והוא משמש במובן של העניין האמיתי המושג בשכל, ולפי המובן השלישי הזה נאמר אצלו יתעלה "<b>תמונה</b>". נאמר: "<b>וּתְמֻנַת ה' יַבִּיט</b>" (במדבר יב,ח), משמעותו וביאורו: ואת אמיתת ה' ישיג.

### root.text.Part 1..3.0 (Main Text)
**English:** Be aware that the three verbs [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), [[t:4]]hibbît[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]הביט[[t:7]]), and [[t:8]]’[[t:9]]hazâ[[t:10]] ([[t:11]]הזה[[t:11]]) are applied to the [Lat.: visus] of the eye; however, they are all employed metaphorically for the perception of the [Lat.: intellectus].
**Correction:** Be aware that the three verbs [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), [[t:4]]hibbît[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]הביט[[t:7]]), and [[t:8]]’[[t:9]]hazâ[[t:10]] ([[t:11]]הזה[[t:11]]) are applied to the vision of the eye; however, they are all employed metaphorically for the perception of the intellect.

**French (Source):** Sache que les trois verbes [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), [[t:4]]hibbît[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]הביט[[t:7]]) et ’[[t:8]]hazâ[[t:9]] ([[t:10]]הזה[[t:11]]) s’appliquent à la [[t:12]]vue[[t:13]] de l’œil; mais on les emploie métaphoriquement, tous les trois, pour la perception de l’intelligence. 

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <b>המונחים ראה, הביט וחזה</b><br><span class="mediumGrey">פתיחה</span><br><sup>1</sup> דע ששלושת הביטויים "<b>ראה</b>" ו"<b>הביט</b>" ו"<b>חזה</b>" משמשים במובן של ראיית העין, ושלושתם הושאלו להשגת השכל.

### root.text.Part 1..3.1 (Main Text)
**English:** With regard to [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), this is well-known to everyone. It is said, for example: [[t:4]]And he saw[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]וירא[[t:7]]), [[t:8]]and behold, there was a well in the field[[t:9]] (Genesis, 29, 2), where it concerns the [Lat.: visus] of the eye; however, in these words: [[t:12]]And my heart saw[[t:13]] ([[t:14]]ראה[[t:15]]) [[t:16]]much wisdom and knowledge[[t:17]] (Ecclesiastes, 1, 16), it concerns an [Lat.: intellectus] perception.
**Correction:** With regard to [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), this is well-known to everyone. It is said, for example: [[t:4]]And he saw[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]וירא[[t:7]]), [[t:8]]and behold, there was a well in the field[[t:9]] (Genesis, 29, 2), where it concerns the vision of the eye; however, in these words: [[t:12]]And my heart saw[[t:13]] ([[t:14]]ראה[[t:15]]) [[t:16]]much wisdom and knowledge[[t:17]] (Ecclesiastes, 1, 16), it concerns an intellect perception.

**French (Source):** Pour [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]), cela est connu à tout le monde. Il est dit, p. ex.: [[t:4]]Et il vit[[t:5]] ([[t:6]]וירא[[t:7]]), [[t:8]]et voici, il y avait un puits dans le champ[[t:9]] (Genèse, 29, 2), où il s’agit de la [[t:10]]vue[[t:11]] de l’œil; mais dans ces mots: [[t:12]]Et mon cœur voyait[[t:13]] ([[t:14]]ראה[[t:15]]) [[t:16]]beaucoup de sagesse et de science[[t:17]] (Ecclésiaste, 1, 16), il s’agit d’une perception intellectuelle. 

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">שתי משמעויות למונח ראה</span><br><sup>2</sup> לגבי "<b>ראה</b>" הדבר ידוע בקרב ההמון. <br><sup>א)</sup> נאמר: "<b>וַיַּרְא וְהִנֵּה בְאֵר בַּשָּׂדֶה</b>" (בראשית כט,ב), וזו ראיית עין; <br><sup>ב)</sup> ונאמר "<b>וְלִבִּי רָאָה הַרְבֵּה חָכְמָה וָדָעַת</b>" (קהלת א,טז), וזו השגה שכלית. 

---

### root.text.Part 1..3.2 (Main Text)
**English:** It is in this metaphorical sense that the verb [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]) must be understood whenever it is applied to God, as for example in these passages: [[t:4]]I[[t:5]] [[t:6]]saw[[t:7]] ([[t:8]]ראיתי[[t:9]]) [[t:10]]the Lord[[t:11]] (I Kings, 22, 19); [[t:12]]And the Lord appeared[[t:13]] [[t:14]]to him[[t:15]] (Genesis, 18, 1); [[t:16]]And God [[t:17]] [[t:18]]saw[[t:19]] [[t:20]]that it was good[[t:21]] (Genesis, 1, [[t:22]]passim[[t:23]]); [[t:24]]Show me[[t:25]] [[t:26]]thy glory[[t:27]] (Exodus, 33, 18); [[t:28]]And they [[t:29]] [[t:30]]saw[[t:31]] [[t:32]]the God of Israel[[t:33]] ([[t:34]]Ibid.[[t:35]], 24, 10). In all these instances, it is a matter of intellectual perception, and not at all of the [Lat.: visus] of the eye; for the eyes only perceive (what is) corporeal and (only) from a certain side[[fn:77]], and with this, some accidents of the body, such as its colours, its geometric figure, etc., and God, on His part, does not perceive by means of an instrument, as will be explained (later).
**Correction:** It is in this metaphorical sense that the verb [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]) must be understood whenever it is applied to God, as for example in these passages: [[t:4]]I[[t:5]] [[t:6]]saw[[t:7]] ([[t:8]]ראיתי[[t:9]]) [[t:10]]the Lord[[t:11]] (I Kings, 22, 19); [[t:12]]And the Lord appeared[[t:13]] [[t:14]]to him[[t:15]] (Genesis, 18, 1); [[t:16]]And God [[t:17]] [[t:18]]saw[[t:19]] [[t:20]]that it was good[[t:21]] (Genesis, 1, [[t:22]]passim[[t:23]]); [[t:24]]Show me[[t:25]] [[t:26]]thy glory[[t:27]] (Exodus, 33, 18); [[t:28]]And they [[t:29]] [[t:30]]saw[[t:31]] [[t:32]]the God of Israel[[t:33]] ([[t:34]]Ibid.[[t:35]], 24, 10). In all these instances, it is a matter of intellectual perception, and not at all of the vision of the eye; for the eyes only perceive (what is) corporeal and (only) from a certain side[[fn:77]], and with this, some accidents of the body, such as its colours, its geometric figure, etc., and God, on His part, does not perceive by means of an instrument, as will be explained (later).

**French (Source):** C’est dans ce sens métaphorique qu’il faut prendre le verbe [[t:0]]raâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]ראה[[t:3]]) toutes les fois qu’il s’applique à Dieu, comme p. ex. dans ces passages: [[t:4]]Je[[t:5]] [[t:6]]VIS[[t:7]] ([[t:8]]ראיתי[[t:9]]) [[t:10]]l’Éternel[[t:11]] (I Rois, 22, 19); [[t:12]]Et l’Éternel se fit[[t:13]] [[t:14]]VOIR[[t:15]] (apparut) [[t:16]]à lui[[t:17]] (Genèse, 18, 1); [[t:18]]Et Dieu[[t:19]] [[t:20]]VIT[[t:21]] [[t:22]]que c’était bien[[t:23]] (Genèse, 1, [[t:24]]passim[[t:25]]); [[t:26]]Fais-moi[[t:27]] [[t:28]]VOIR[[t:29]] [[t:30]]ta gloire[[t:31]] Exod., 33, 18); [[t:32]]Et ils[[t:33]] [[t:34]]VIRENT[[t:35]] [[t:36]]le Dieu d’Israël[[t:37]] ([[t:38]]Ibid.[[t:39]], 24, 10). Il s’agit ici partout d’une perception intellectuelle, et nullement de la [[t:40]]vue[[t:41]] de l’œil; car les yeux ne perçoivent que (ce qui est) corps et (seulement) d’un certain côté[[fn:77]], et avec cela quelques accidents du corps, tels que ses couleurs, sa figure (géométrique), etc., et Dieu, de son côté, ne perçoit pas au moyen d’un instrument, comme on l’expliquera (plus loin).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** ובהשאלה הזאת כל <b>לשון ראיה</b> שנאמר לגבי ה' יתעלה, כגון דבריו "<b>רָאִיתִי אֶת ה'</b>" (מלכים־א כב,יט; דברי הימים־ב יח,יח), "<b>וַיֵּרָא אֵלָיו ה'</b>" (בראשית יח,א), "<b>וַיַּרְא אֱלֹהִים כִּי טוֹב</b>" (בראשית א,י ועוד), "<b>הַרְאֵנִי נָא אֶת כְּבֹדֶךָ</b>" (שמות לג,יח), "<b>וַיִּרְאוּ אֵת אֱלֹהֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל</b>" (שם,כד,י) – כל אלה השגה שכלית, לא ראיית עין כלל. משום שהעיניים אינן משיגות אלא גוף, וגם זה רק מכיוון אחד ורק חלק מן המקרים (=התכונות) שלו, כלומר צבע הגוף ותבניתו וכיוצא בזה, וכן הוא יתעלה אינו משיג על ידי אבר, כמו שיתבאר (א,מד; מו18,6).

### root.text.Part 1..3.3 (Main Text)
**English:** Similarly, [[t:0]]hibbît[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]הביט[[t:3]]) is used in the sense of: [[t:4]]looking[[t:5]] at a thing with the eye; for example: [[t:6]]Do not look[[t:7]] [[t:8]]אל תבט[[t:9]] [[t:10]]behind thee[[t:11]] (Genesis, 19, 17); [[t:12]]And his wife looked[[t:13]] [[t:14]]ותבט[[t:15]], [[t:16]]being behind him[[t:17]] ([[t:18]]Ibid.[[t:19]], [[t:20]]V[[t:21]]. 26); [[t:22]]And he shall look[[t:23]] [[t:24]]ונבט[[t:25]] [[t:26]]toward the earth[[t:27]] (Isaiah, 5, 30). But it is used metaphorically for the [Lat.: intuitus] of the mind approaching the consideration of a thing in order to understand it, as in this passage: [[t:28]]No iniquity is seen[[t:29]] [[t:30]]לא הביט[[t:31]] [[t:32]]in Jacob[[t:33]] (Numbers, 23, 21); for iniquity is not seen with the eye. It is the same with these words: [[t:34]]And they looked[[t:35]] [[t:36]]והביטו[[t:37]] [[t:38]]after Moses[[t:39]] (Exodus, 33, 8); for, according to what the doctors say, they express the same idea, and they state that they (the Israelites) were watching his actions and words and examining them[[fn:78]]. The words: [[t:40]]Look[[t:41]] [[t:42]]הבט[[t:43]] [[t:44]]toward the heaven[[t:45]] (Genesis, 15, 5), have the same meaning; for this took place in a prophetic vision. And (in general) the verb [[t:46]]הביט[[t:47]] (to look) has this metaphorical sense whenever it is applied to God; for example: … [[t:48]]to look[[t:49]] [[t:50]]מהביט[[t:51]] [[t:52]]toward God[[t:53]] (Exodus, 3, 6); [[t:54]]And he contemplates[[t:55]] [[t:56]]יביט[[t:57]] [[t:58]]the figure of God[[t:59]] (Numbers, 12, 8); [[t:60]]And thou canst not look[[t:61]] [[t:62]]והביט[[t:63]] [[t:64]]at iniquity[[t:65]] (Habakkuk, 1, 13).
**Correction:** Similarly, [[t:0]]hibbît[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]הביט[[t:3]]) is used in the sense of: [[t:4]]looking[[t:5]] at a thing with the eye; for example: [[t:6]]Do not look[[t:7]] [[t:8]]אל תבט[[t:9]] [[t:10]]behind thee[[t:11]] (Genesis, 19, 17); [[t:12]]And his wife looked[[t:13]] [[t:14]]ותבט[[t:15]], [[t:16]]being behind him[[t:17]] ([[t:18]]Ibid.[[t:19]], [[t:20]]V[[t:21]]. 26); [[t:22]]And he shall look[[t:23]] [[t:24]]ונבט[[t:25]] [[t:26]]toward the earth[[t:27]] (Isaiah, 5, 30). But it is used metaphorically for theintuition of the mind approaching the consideration of a thing in order to understand it, as in this passage: [[t:28]]No iniquity is seen[[t:29]] [[t:30]]לא הביט[[t:31]] [[t:32]]in Jacob[[t:33]] (Numbers, 23, 21); for iniquity is not seen with the eye. It is the same with these words: [[t:34]]And they looked[[t:35]] [[t:36]]והביטו[[t:37]] [[t:38]]after Moses[[t:39]] (Exodus, 33, 8); for, according to what the doctors say, they express the same idea, and they state that they (the Israelites) were watching his actions and words and examining them[[fn:78]]. The words: [[t:40]]Look[[t:41]] [[t:42]]הבט[[t:43]] [[t:44]]toward the heaven[[t:45]] (Genesis, 15, 5), have the same meaning; for this took place in a prophetic vision. And (in general) the verb [[t:46]]הביט[[t:47]] (to look) has this metaphorical sense whenever it is applied to God; for example: … [[t:48]]to look[[t:49]] [[t:50]]מהביט[[t:51]] [[t:52]]toward God[[t:53]] (Exodus, 3, 6); [[t:54]]And he contemplates[[t:55]] [[t:56]]יביט[[t:57]] [[t:58]]the figure of God[[t:59]] (Numbers, 12, 8); [[t:60]]And thou canst not look[[t:61]] [[t:62]]והביט[[t:63]] [[t:64]]at iniquity[[t:65]] (Habakkuk, 1, 13).

**French (Source):** De même [[t:0]]hibbît[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]הביט[[t:3]]) s’emploie dans le sens de: [[t:4]]regarder[[t:5]] une chose avec l’œil; p. ex.: [[t:6]]Ne regarde pas[[t:7]] ([[t:8]]אל תבט[[t:9]]) [[t:10]]derrière toi[[t:11]] (Genèse, 19, 17): [[t:12]]Et sa femme regarda[[t:13]] ([[t:14]]ותבט[[t:15]]), [[t:16]]étant derrière lui[[t:17]] ([[t:18]]Ibid.[[t:19]], [[t:20]]V[[t:21]]. 26); [[t:22]]Et il regardera[[t:23]] ([[t:24]]ונבט[[t:25]]) [[t:26]]vers la terre[[t:27]] (Isaïe, 5, 30). Mais on l’emploie métaphoriquement pour le [[t:28]]regard[[t:29]] de l’esprit abordant la considération d’une chose pour la comprendre, comme dans ce passage: [[t:30]]On ne voit pas[[t:31]] ([[t:32]]לא הביט[[t:33]]) [[t:34]]d’iniquité dans Jacob[[t:35]] (Nombres, 23, 21); car l’iniquité ne se voit pas avec l’œil. Il en est de même de ces paroles: [[t:36]]Et ils regardèrent[[t:37]] ([[t:38]]והביטו[[t:39]]) [[t:40]]après Moïse[[t:41]] (Exod., 33, 8); car, selon ce que disent les docteurs, elles exprimeraient la même idée, et elles énonceraient qu’ils (les Israélites) épiaient ses actes et ses paroles et les examinaient[[fn:78]]. Les mots: [[t:42]]Regarde[[t:43]] ([[t:44]]הבט[[t:45]]) [[t:46]]donc vers le ciel[[t:47]] (Genèse, 15, 5), ont encore le même sens; car cela se passait dans une vision prophétique. Et (en général) le verbe [[t:48]]הביט[[t:49]] (regarder) a ce sens métaphorique toutes les fois qu’il s’applique à Dieu; p. ex.: ….. [[t:50]]de regarder[[t:51]] ([[t:52]]מהביט[[t:53]]) [[t:54]]vers Dieu[[t:55]] (Exod., 3, 6); [[t:56]]Et il contemple[[t:57]] ([[t:58]]יביט[[t:59]]) [[t:60]]la figure de Dieu[[t:61]] (Nombres, 12, 8); [[t:62]]Et tu ne peux regarder[[t:63]] ([[t:64]]והביט[[t:65]]) [[t:66]]l’iniquité[[t:67]] (Habac., I, 13).

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">שתי משמעויות למונח הביט</span><br><sup>3</sup> וכיוצא בזה "<b>הביט</b>" <br><sup>א)</sup> משמש במובן של הפניית העין אל דבר: "<b>אַל תַּבִּיט אַחֲרֶיךָ</b>" (בראשית יט,יז), "<b>וַתַּבֵּט אִשְׁתּוֹ מֵאַחֲרָיו</b>" (שם,כו), "<b>וְנִבַּט לָאָרֶץ</b>" (ישעיהו ה,ל). <br><sup>ב)</sup> והושאל לפניית הדעת והכוונתה לעיין בדבר כדי שתשיג אותו, והוא שנאמר: "<b>לֹא הִבִּיט אָוֶן בְּיַעֲקֹב</b>" (במדבר כג,כא), כי ה<b>אָוֶן</b> אינו נראה בעין. וכיוצא בזה דבריו: "<b>וְהִבִּיטוּ אַחֲרֵי מֹשֶׁה</b> [עַד בֹּאוֹ הָאֹהֱלָה]" (שמות לג,ח), אמרו ה<b>חכמים ז"ל</b> שיש בו גם המשמעות הזו, ושזו הודעה על כך שהם עקבו אחרי מעשיו ודבריו והתבוננו בהם. במשמעות זו נאמר: "<b>הַבֶּט נָא הַשָּׁמַיְמָה</b>" (בראשית טו,ה), משום שזה היה <b>במראה הנבואה</b>. ובהשאלה זו כל <b>לשון הבטה</b> שנאמר לגבי האל יתעלה: "[כִּי יָרֵא] <b>מֵהַבִּיט אֶל הָאֱלֹהִים</b>" (שמות ג,ו), "<b>וּתְמֻנַת ה' יַבִּיט</b>" (במדבר יב,ח), "<b>וְהַבִּיט אֶל עָמָל לֹא תוּכָל</b>" (חבקוק א,יג).

### root.text.Part 1..3.4 (Main Text)
**English:** Similarly, ’[[t:0]]hazâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]חזה[[t:3]]) applies to the [Lat.: visus] of the eye; for example: [[t:4]]And our eyes shall see[[t:5]] [[t:6]]ותחז[[t:7]] [[t:8]]the fall of Zion[[t:9]] (Micah, 4, 11); and it is used metaphorically for the perception of the heart, for example: … [[t:10]]he saw[[t:11]] [[t:12]]חזה[[t:13]] [[t:14]]upon Judah and Jerusalem[[t:15]] (Isaiah, 1, 1); … [[t:16]]the word of the Lord to Abram[[t:17]] [[t:18]]במחזה[[t:19]] [[t:20]]in a vision[[t:21]] (Genesis, 15, 1); and according to this metaphor it has been said: [[t:22]]And they saw[[t:23]] [[t:24]]ויחזו[[t:25]] [[t:26]]God[[t:27]] (Exodus, 24, 11). You must thoroughly understand this.
**Correction:** Similarly, ’[[t:0]]hazâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]חזה[[t:3]]) applies to the vision of the eye; for example: [[t:4]]And our eyes shall see[[t:5]] [[t:6]]ותחז[[t:7]] [[t:8]]the fall of Zion[[t:9]] (Micah, 4, 11); and it is used metaphorically for the perception of the heart, for example: … [[t:10]]he saw[[t:11]] [[t:12]]חזה[[t:13]] [[t:14]]upon Judah and Jerusalem[[t:15]] (Isaiah, 1, 1); … [[t:16]]the word of the Lord to Abram[[t:17]] [[t:18]]במחזה[[t:19]] [[t:20]]in a vision[[t:21]] (Genesis, 15, 1); and according to this metaphor it has been said: [[t:22]]And they saw[[t:23]] [[t:24]]ויחזו[[t:25]] [[t:26]]God[[t:27]] (Exodus, 24, 11). You must thoroughly understand this.

**French (Source):** De même ’[[t:0]]hazâ[[t:1]] ([[t:2]]חזה[[t:3]]) s’applique à la [[t:4]]vue[[t:5]] de l’œil; p. ex.: [[t:6]]Et que nos yeux voient[[t:7]] ([[t:8]]ותחז[[t:9]]) [[t:10]]la chute de Sion[[t:11]] (Micha, 4, 11); et on l’emploie métaphoriquement pour la perception du cœur, p. ex.: … [[t:12]]qu’il vit[[t:13]] ([[t:14]]חזה[[t:15]]) [[t:16]]sur Juda et Jérusalem[[t:17]] (Isaïe, 1, 1); … [[t:18]]la parole de l’Éternel à Abrâm[[t:19]] ([[t:20]]במחזה[[t:21]]) [[t:22]]dans une vision[[t:23]] (Genèse, 15, 1); et selon cette métaphore il a été dit: [[t:24]]Et ils virent[[t:25]] ([[t:26]]ויחזו[[t:27]]) [[t:28]]Dieu[[t:29]] (Exod., 24, 11). Il faut bien te pénétrer de cela.

**Hebrew (Makbili):** <span class="mediumGrey">שתי משמעויות למונח חזה</span><br><sup>4</sup> וכיוצא בזה "<b>חזה</b>" <br><sup>א)</sup> חל על ראיית העין: "<b>וְתַחַז בְּצִיּוֹן עֵינֵינוּ</b>" (מיכה ד,יא). <br><sup>ב)</sup> והושאל להשגת הלב: "<b>אֲשֶׁר חָזָה עַל יְהוּדָה וִירוּשָׁלִָם</b>" (ישעיהו א,א), "<b>הָיָה דְבַר ה' אֶל אַבְרָם בַּמַּחֲזֶה</b>" (בראשית טו,א). ובהשאלה זו נאמר "<b>וַיֶּחֱזוּ אֶת הָאֱלֹהִים</b>" (שמות כד,יא). דע זאת.
