### root.text.Part 1..45.7.sub_0 (Main Text)
**English:** As touching the organs of locomotion ascribed unto God, it is said, for example: <i>The footstool of my feet</i> (Isaiah lxvi. 1); <i>And the place of the soles of my feet</i> (Ezek. xliii. 7). As for the organs of touch ascribed unto God, there is said: <i>The hand of the Lord</i> (Exod. ix. 3 et <i>passim</i>); <i>With the finger of God</i> (<i>Ibid.</i> xxxi. 18); <i>The work of thy fingers</i> (Ps. viii. 4); <i>And thou hast laid thine hand upon me</i> (<i>Ibid.</i> cxxxix. 5); <i>And to whom is the arm of the Lord revealed</i> (Isaiah liii. 1); <i>Thy right hand, O Lord</i> (Exod. xv. 6). Unto Him have been ascribed the organs of speech, it being said: <i>The mouth of the Lord hath spoken</i> (Isaiah i. 20 et <i>passim</i>); … <i>and that he would open his lips against thee</i> (Job xi. 5); <i>The voice of the Lord is powerful</i> (Ps. xxix. 4); <i>And his tongue as a devouring fire</i> (Isaiah xxx. 27). Finally, there are ascribed unto Him the organs of sensation, it being said: <i>His eyes behold, his eyelids try the children of men</i> (Ps. xi. 4); <i>The eyes of the Lord run to and fro</i> (Zechariah iv. 10)[[fn:390]]; <i>Incline thine ear, O Lord, and hear</i> (2 Kings xix. 16); <i>Ye have kindled a fire in mine anger</i> (Jeremiah xvii. 4). Of the inward members, none have been ascribed unto Him save the heart, because the name of the <i>heart</i> is a homonym signifying also <i>intellect</i>[[fn:391]], and because the heart is the principle of life in the living being. Verily, by these expressions: <i>My bowels are troubled for him</i> (Jeremiah xxxi. 20); <i>The sounding of thy bowels</i> (Isaiah lxiii. 15), it was likewise intended to designate <i>the heart</i>; for <i>bowels</i>[[fn:392]] is a term employed in both a general and a particular sense, signifying in particular the <i>intestines</i>, and in general any <i>inward member</i>, and consequently the <i>heart</i> also. The proof whereof is that the expression hath been used: <i>And thy Law is</i> <small>WITHIN MY BOWELS</small> (Ps. xl. 9) as equivalent to: <i>in my heart</i>.
**Correction:** As touching the organs of locomotion ascribed unto God, it is said, for example: <i>The footstool of my feet</i> (Isaiah lxvi. 1); <i>And the place of the soles of my feet</i> (Ezek. xliii. 7). As for the organs of touch ascribed unto God, there is said: <i>The hand of the Lord</i> (Exod. ix. 3 et <i>passim</i>); <i>With the finger of God</i> (<i>Ibid.</i> xxxi. 18); <i>The work of thy fingers</i> (Ps. viii. 4); <i>And thou hast laid thine hand upon me</i> (<i>Ibid.</i> cxxxix. 5); <i>And to whom is the arm of the Lord revealed</i> (Isaiah liii. 1); <i>Thy right hand, O Lord</i> (Exod. xv. 6). Unto Him have been ascribed the organs of speech, it being said: <i>The mouth of the Lord hath spoken</i> (Isaiah i. 20 et <i>passim</i>); … <i>and that he would open his lips against thee</i> (Job xi. 5); <i>The voice of the Lord is powerful</i> (Ps. xxix. 4); <i>And his tongue as a devouring fire</i> (Isaiah xxx. 27). Finally, there are ascribed unto Him the organs of sensation, it being said: <i>His eyes behold, his eyelids try the children of men</i> (Ps. xi. 4); <i>The eyes of the Lord run to and fro</i> (Zechariah iv. 10)[[fn:390]]; <i>Incline thine ear, O Lord, and hear</i> (2 Kings xix. 16); <i>Ye have kindled a fire in mine anger</i> (Jeremiah xvii. 4). Of the inward members, none have been ascribed unto Him save the heart, because the name of the <i>heart</i> is a homonym signifying also <i>intellect</i>[[fn:391]], and because the heart is the principle of life in the living being. Verily, by these expressions: <i>My bowels are troubled for him</i> (Jeremiah xxxi. 20); <i>The sounding of thy bowels</i> (Isaiah lxiii. 15), it was likewise intended to designate <i>the heart</i>; for <i>bowels</i>[[fn:392]] is a term employed in both a general and a particular sense, signifying in particular the <i>intestines</i>, and in general any <i>inward member</i>, and consequently the <i>heart</i> also. The proof whereof is that the expression hath been used: <i>And thy Law is</i> <small>WITHIN MY BOWELS</small> (Ps. xl. 9) as equivalent to: <i>in my heart</i>.

**French (Source):** Pour ce qui est des organes de locomotion attribués à Dieu, on dit, p. ex.: [[t:0]]Le marche-pied de mes pieds[[t:1]] (Isaïe, 66, 1); [[t:2]]Et le lieu des plantes de mes pieds[[t:3]] (Hézek., XLIII, 7). Quant aux organes de tact attribués à Dieu (on dit, p. ex.): [[t:4]]La main de l’Éternel[[t:5]] (Exode, 9, 3 et [[t:6]]passim[[t:7]]); [[t:8]]Du doigt de Dieu[[t:9]] ([[t:10]]Ibid.[[t:11]], 31, 18); [[t:12]]L’ouvrage de tes doigts[[t:13]] (Ps. 8, 4); [[t:14]]Et tu as mis sur moi la paume de ta main[[t:15]] ([[t:16]]Ibid.[[t:17]] 139, 5); [[t:18]]Et sur qui se manifestait le bras de l’Éternel[[t:19]] (Isaïe, 53, 1); [[t:20]]Ta droite, ô Éternel[[t:21]] (Exode, 15, 6). On lui a attribué les organes de la parole (en disant): [[t:22]]La bouche de l’Éternel a parlé[[t:23]] (Isaïe, 1, 20 et [[t:24]]passim[[t:25]]); … [[t:26]]et qu’il ouvrît ses lèvres avec toi[[t:27]] (Job, 11, 5); [[t:28]]La voix de l’Éternel, avec force[[t:29]] (Ps. 29, 4); [[t:30]]Et sa langue est comme un feu dévorant[[t:31]] (Isaïe, 30, 27). Enfin, on lui a attribué les organes de sensation en disant: [[t:32]]Ses yeux voient, ses paupières sondent les fils d’Adam[[t:33]] (Ps. 11, 4); [[t:34]]Les yeux de l’Éternel parcourent[[t:35]] (Zacharie, 4, 10)[[fn:390]]; [[t:36]]Incline, ô Éternel, ton oreille et écoute[[t:37]] (II Rois, 19, 16); [[t:38]]Vous avez allumé un feu dans mon nez[[t:39]] (Jérémie, 17, 5). Des membres intérieurs, on ne lui en a attribué que le cœur, parce que le nom (du [[t:40]]cœur[[t:41]]) est un homonyme qui signifie aussi [[t:42]]intelligence[[t:43]][[fn:391]], et parce que (le cœur) est le principe de vie de l’être vivant. En effet, par ces expressions: [[t:44]]Mes entrailles ont gémi pour lui[[t:45]] (Jérémie, 31, 20); [[t:46]]Le gémissement de tes entrailles[[t:47]] (Isaïe, 63, 15), on a voulu également désigner [[t:48]]le cœur;[[t:49]] car [[t:50]]entrailles[[t:51]][[fn:392]] est un nom qui s’emploie dans un sens général et dans un sens particulier, désignant en particulier les [[t:52]]intestins[[t:53]], et en général tout [[t:54]]membre intérieur[[t:55]], et par conséquent aussi le [[t:56]]cœur[[t:57]]. Ce qui en est la preuve, c’est qu’on a employé l’expression: [[t:58]]Et ta Loi est[[t:59]] [[t:60]]DANS MES ENTRAILLES[[t:61]] (Ps. 40, 9) comme équivalent de: [[t:62]]dans mon cœur[[t:63]].

**Hebrew (Makbili):** [Not Found]
